It is observed that the transmission parameters \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta _v$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta _h$$\end{document} have the strongest positive influence, while mortality rates and delay parameters contribute to reducing the reproduction number.

Overall, the sensitivity analysis confirms that transmission rates and delay parameters play a critical role in shaping the disease dynamics, which is consistent with the numerical simulations presented in the previous section.

Numerical simulations and discussions

This section includes numerical simulations to explain and verify the analytical findings established in the earlier sections. Particularly, we study the threshold dynamics related to the basic reproduction number \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0$$\end{document} and the impact of time delay on the transmission dynamics of the model.

All simulations have been carried out by applying the suggested NSFD scheme which preserves the dynamical characteristics of the continuous model.

Threshold dynamics validation

To validate the mathematical expression for the basic reproduction number, two cases have been considered.

Case 1: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0<1$$\end{document}. Under this case, the results of the numerical simulations demonstrate that the infected populations, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I_{b1}(t)$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I_h(t)$$\end{document}, tend to converge towards zero with increasing time.

Case 2: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0>1$$\end{document}. Under this case, the infected populations exist and converge to a steady state, hence the presence of endemic equilibrium.

The above conclusions are in line with the threshold conditions formulated for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0$$\end{document}.

Effect of delays on dynamics

In order to study the effect of the delays, we let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _h$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _v$$\end{document} be variable while leaving all other parameters fixed.

It is clear from the numerical simulations that when the values of the delays increase, the dynamics of the system is greatly influenced. More precisely, higher delays slow down the rate of reaching equilibrium, and in certain cases even produce oscillations among the infected groups.

Moreover, an increase in delays causes a decrease in the effective transmission because of the exponential term \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}$$\end{document}, which consequently results in a reduction in the size of infection.

These facts support the theory about the damping role of delays during transmission.

Analysis on delay parameters’ sensitivity

To investigate the importance of delays further, we carry out a sensitivity analysis on the delay parameters \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _h$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _v$$\end{document} in their biologically realistic range of variation.

The analysis reveals that the value of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0$$\end{document} decreases with an increase in delay parameter values due to the decreased likelihood of survival in the delay period. Therefore, increasing delays can be beneficial in reducing infection spread.

Hence, it is evident that the delay parameters have a significant impact on the overall dynamics of the model.

Design of the NSFD schemes

In this section, we construct the first-order nonstandard finite difference (NSFD) method and the second-order NSFD scheme for the delayed canine Chagas disease model (1)–(7).

Formulation of the first-order NSFD method

In this subsection, we construct a first-order nonstandard finite difference (NSFD) scheme for the delayed canine Chagas disease model. The construction follows the NSFD methodology introduced by Mickens29, in which the classical denominator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta t$$\end{document} is replaced by a nonstandard denominator function and nonlinear terms are approximated nonlocally so as to preserve the qualitative properties of the continuous system. Such an approach has been successfully used in epidemiological modeling (see,30–32). Such an approach is particularly suitable for long-time simulations, where standard finite difference methods may generate spurious oscillations or biologically meaningless negative populations.

We consider the uniform partition of the time interval [0, T] given by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0=t_0<t_1<\cdots <t_N=T, \qquad t_k=k\Delta t, \quad \Delta t>0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Let\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^k \approx E(t_k), \qquad Y^k \approx Y(t_k), \qquad S_{b1}^k \approx S_{b1}(t_k), \qquad I_{b1}^k \approx I_{b1}(t_k), \\ S_{b2}^k \approx S_{b2}(t_k), \qquad S_h^k \approx S_h(t_k), \qquad I_h^k \approx I_h(t_k) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}denote the discrete approximations of the state variables. Furthermore, define the total vector and host populations by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_b^k=S_{b1}^k+I_{b1}^k+S_{b2}^k, \qquad N_{b1}^k=S_{b1}^k+I_{b1}^k, \qquad N_h^k=S_h^k+I_h^k. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}To incorporate the biological delays associated with host-to-vector and vector-to-host transmission, we introduce the delay indices\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} m_h=\frac{\tau _h}{\Delta t}, \qquad m_v=\frac{\tau _v}{\Delta t}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}A general finite difference approximation of the continuous model may be written in the abstract form\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} D_h(X^k)=F_h(f:X^k), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D_h(X^k)$$\end{document} approximates the time derivative of the state vector at \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t=t_k$$\end{document}, while \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_h(f:X^k)$$\end{document} approximates the right-hand side of the continuous delayed system. Before presenting the scheme, we recall the following standard definitions.

Definition 1

A finite difference method for the delayed canine Chagas disease model is said to be positive if, for any step size \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta t>0$$\end{document} and any nonnegative initial discrete history, its numerical solution remains nonnegative for all time levels30,31. That is, if\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} X^k \in {\mathbb {R}}_+^7 \quad \text {for all prescribed initial indices}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} X^k \in {\mathbb {R}}_+^7 \quad \text {for all } k \ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Definition 2

A finite difference method is said to be elementary stable if, for any step size \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta t>0$$\end{document}, its equilibrium points coincide with those of the continuous differential system, and the local stability properties of each equilibrium are preserved by the discrete scheme31,32.

Definition 3

A one-step finite difference method is called a nonstandard finite difference (NSFD) method if at least one of the following conditions is satisfied29,30: the discrete derivative is written in the form \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{X^{k+1}-X^k}{\phi (\Delta t)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi (\Delta t)$$\end{document} is a nonnegative denominator function satisfying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \phi (\Delta t)=\Delta t+{\mathcal {O}}(\Delta t^2), \qquad \Delta t \rightarrow 0; \end{aligned}$$\end{document}nonlinear terms are approximated nonlocally in such a way that the essential dynamical properties of the continuous model are preserved.

the discrete derivative is written in the form \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{X^{k+1}-X^k}{\phi (\Delta t)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi (\Delta t)$$\end{document} is a nonnegative denominator function satisfying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \phi (\Delta t)=\Delta t+{\mathcal {O}}(\Delta t^2), \qquad \Delta t \rightarrow 0; \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

the discrete derivative is written in the form \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{X^{k+1}-X^k}{\phi (\Delta t)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi (\Delta t)$$\end{document} is a nonnegative denominator function satisfying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \phi (\Delta t)=\Delta t+{\mathcal {O}}(\Delta t^2), \qquad \Delta t \rightarrow 0; \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

nonlinear terms are approximated nonlocally in such a way that the essential dynamical properties of the continuous model are preserved.

nonlinear terms are approximated nonlocally in such a way that the essential dynamical properties of the continuous model are preserved.

In the present model, the classical denominator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta t$$\end{document} is replaced by denominator functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _i(\Delta t)$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i=1,\dots ,7$$\end{document}, satisfying the consistency condition\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \phi _i(\Delta t)=\Delta t+{\mathcal {O}}(\Delta t^2), \qquad \Delta t \rightarrow 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Moreover, the nonlinear incidence terms are discretized nonlocally by evaluating one factor at the future level and the remaining factors at the current or delayed levels. This construction guarantees that the resulting scheme preserves positivity and remains dynamically consistent with the continuous delayed model.

The first-order NSFD scheme for the delayed canine Chagas disease model is given by11\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & \frac{E^{k+1}-E^k}{\phi _1(\Delta t)} = \lambda N_b^k-(\tau +\delta _e)E^{k+1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}12\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & \frac{Y^{k+1}-Y^k}{\phi _2(\Delta t)} = \tau E^k\left( 1-\frac{Y^k+N_b^k}{\kappa }\right) -(\gamma _y+\delta _y)Y^{k+1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}13\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & \frac{S_{b1}^{k+1}-S_{b1}^k}{\phi _3(\Delta t)} = \varphi \gamma _yY^k-\beta _v S_{b1}^{k+1}\frac{I_h^{k-m_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{N_h^k}-\delta _b S_{b1}^{k+1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}14\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & \frac{I_{b1}^{k+1}-I_{b1}^k}{\phi _4(\Delta t)} = \beta _v S_{b1}^k\frac{I_h^{k-m_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{N_h^k}-\delta _b I_{b1}^{k+1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}15\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & \frac{S_{b2}^{k+1}-S_{b2}^k}{\phi _5(\Delta t)} = (1-\varphi )\gamma _yY^k-\delta _b S_{b2}^{k+1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}16\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & \frac{S_h^{k+1}-S_h^k}{\phi _6(\Delta t)} = \delta _d N_h^k-\beta _h S_h^{k+1}\frac{I_{b1}^{k-m_v}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{N_{b1}^k}-\delta _d S_h^{k+1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}17\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & \frac{I_h^{k+1}-I_h^k}{\phi _7(\Delta t)} = \beta _h S_h^k\frac{I_{b1}^{k-m_v}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{N_{b1}^k}-\delta _d I_h^{k+1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}The discrete system (11)–(17) represents the first-order NSFD approximation of the delayed canine Chagas disease model. The use of denominator functions together with nonlocal approximations of the nonlinear transmission terms ensures that the numerical method preserves the essential qualitative features of the continuous model, especially positivity and dynamical consistency.

Qualitative properties of first order NSFD scheme

In this subsection, we study some qualitative properties of the proposed first order NSFD scheme (11)–(17). In particular, we show that the scheme preserves the positivity of the numerical solutions.

Theorem 4

Assume that the initial discrete history is nonnegative, that is,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^k,\,Y^k,\,S_{b1}^k,\,I_{b1}^k,\,S_{b2}^k,\,S_h^k,\,I_h^k \ge 0 \end{aligned}$$\end{document}for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k=-m,\ldots ,0$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m=\max (m_h,m_v)$$\end{document}. Then the numerical solution generated by the first-order NSFD scheme (11)–(17) remains nonnegative for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k\ge 0$$\end{document}.

Proof

From the first-order NSFD scheme (11)–(17), we can express the discrete variables explicitly.

From Eq. (11) we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{E^{k+1}-E^k}{\phi _1(\Delta t)} = \lambda N_b^k-(\tau +\delta _e)E^{k+1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Multiplying both sides by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _1(\Delta t)$$\end{document} gives\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1}-E^k = \phi _1(\Delta t)\lambda N_b^k - \phi _1(\Delta t)(\tau +\delta _e)E^{k+1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Rearranging the terms, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1} + \phi _1(\Delta t)(\tau +\delta _e)E^{k+1} = E^k + \phi _1(\Delta t)\lambda N_b^k. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Thus\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1} \left[ 1+\phi _1(\Delta t)(\tau +\delta _e)\right] = E^k+\phi _1(\Delta t)\lambda N_b^k. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hence\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1} = \frac{E^k+\phi _1(\Delta t)\lambda N_b^k}{1+\phi _1(\Delta t)(\tau +\delta _e)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^k\ge 0$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N_b^k\ge 0$$\end{document}, and the denominator is positive, it follows that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1}\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Next, from Eq. (12) we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{Y^{k+1}-Y^k}{\phi _2(\Delta t)} = \tau E^k\left( 1-\frac{Y^k+N_b^k}{\kappa }\right) - (\gamma _y+\delta _y)Y^{k+1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Multiplying both sides by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _2(\Delta t)$$\end{document} gives\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} Y^{k+1}-Y^k = \phi _2(\Delta t)\tau E^k\left( 1-\frac{Y^k+N_b^k}{\kappa }\right) - \phi _2(\Delta t)(\gamma _y+\delta _y)Y^{k+1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Rearranging terms,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} Y^{k+1} + \phi _2(\Delta t)(\gamma _y+\delta _y)Y^{k+1} = Y^k + \phi _2(\Delta t)\tau E^k\left( 1-\frac{Y^k+N_b^k}{\kappa }\right) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hence\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} Y^{k+1} \left[ 1+\phi _2(\Delta t)(\gamma _y+\delta _y)\right] = Y^k + \phi _2(\Delta t)\tau E^k\left( 1-\frac{Y^k+N_b^k}{\kappa }\right) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Therefore\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} Y^{k+1} = \frac{Y^k+\phi _2(\Delta t)\tau E^k\left( 1-\dfrac{Y^k+N_b^k}{\kappa }\right) }{1+\phi _2(\Delta t)(\gamma _y+\delta _y)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Under the biologically feasible region\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0\le Y^k+N_b^k\le \kappa , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}the numerator is nonnegative and the denominator is positive. Hence\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} Y^{k+1}\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}From Eq. (13) we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{S_{b1}^{k+1}-S_{b1}^k}{\phi _3(\Delta t)} = \varphi \gamma _yY^k - \beta _v S_{b1}^{k+1}\frac{I_h^{k-m_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{N_h^k} - \delta _b S_{b1}^{k+1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Multiplying by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _3(\Delta t)$$\end{document},\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} S_{b1}^{k+1}-S_{b1}^k = \phi _3(\Delta t)\varphi \gamma _yY^k - \phi _3(\Delta t)\beta _v S_{b1}^{k+1}\frac{I_h^{k-m_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{N_h^k} - \phi _3(\Delta t)\delta _b S_{b1}^{k+1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Rearranging terms,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} S_{b1}^{k+1} \left[ 1+\phi _3(\Delta t) \left( \beta _v\frac{I_h^{k-m_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{N_h^k} +\delta _b \right) \right] = S_{b1}^k+\phi _3(\Delta t)\varphi \gamma _yY^k. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Thus\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} S_{b1}^{k+1} = \frac{S_{b1}^k+\phi _3(\Delta t)\varphi \gamma _yY^k}{1+\phi _3(\Delta t)\left( \beta _v\dfrac{I_h^{k-m_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{N_h^k}+\delta _b\right) }. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Since the numerator is nonnegative and the denominator is positive, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} S_{b1}^{k+1}\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Using the same procedure for Eqs. (14), (15), (16), and (17), we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} I_{b1}^{k+1}\ge 0,\qquad S_{b2}^{k+1}\ge 0,\qquad S_h^{k+1}\ge 0,\qquad I_h^{k+1}\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Therefore, if all initial values are nonnegative, then all state variables remain nonnegative for every time step k. Hence, the first-order NSFD scheme preserves positivity. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Lemma 2

Let\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Omega =\left\{ (E^k,Y^k,S_{b1}^k,I_{b1}^k,S_{b2}^k,S_h^k,I_h^k)\in {\mathbb {R}}_+^7: S_h^k+I_h^k=N_h^0,\quad Y^k+N_b^k\le \kappa \right\} , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_b^k=S_{b1}^k+I_{b1}^k+S_{b2}^k. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}If the initial discrete history belongs to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega$$\end{document}, then the numerical solution generated by the first-order NSFD scheme (11)–(17) remains in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega$$\end{document} for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k\ge 0$$\end{document}. That is, the set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega$$\end{document} is positively invariant.

Proof

From the positivity theorem, all state variables remain nonnegative for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k\ge 0$$\end{document}. Hence, it remains to show that the defining bounds of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega$$\end{document} are preserved.

First, consider the total host population\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_h^k=S_h^k+I_h^k. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Using Eqs. (16) and (17), and adding the two relations, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_h^{k+1}=N_h^k. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Therefore,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_h^k=N_h^0 \qquad \text {for all } k\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Thus, the host component of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega$$\end{document} is invariant.

Next, consider the quantity\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} W^k=Y^k+N_b^k. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Using the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N_b^k$$\end{document}, we write\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} W^k=Y^k+S_{b1}^k+I_{b1}^k+S_{b2}^k. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}From Eqs. (12)–(15), the evolution of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W^k$$\end{document} is governed by recruitment from the egg compartment together with mortality terms in the immature and adult vector classes. The recruitment term in the nymph equation contains the logistic factor\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\frac{Y^k+N_b^k}{\kappa } = 1-\frac{W^k}{\kappa }. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}If\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} W^k=\kappa , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\frac{W^k}{\kappa }=0, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}and therefore no further growth occurs through the recruitment term. If\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} W^k>\kappa , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\frac{W^k}{\kappa }<0, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}so that the logistic contribution becomes nonpositive, while the mortality terms remain nonnegative losses. Consequently, the dynamics force the solution back toward the region\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} W^k\le \kappa . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hence, whenever the initial data satisfy\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} Y^k+N_b^k\le \kappa , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}this inequality is preserved for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k\ge 0$$\end{document}. Thus\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} W^k=Y^k+N_b^k\le \kappa \qquad \text {for all } k\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Combining the nonnegativity of the numerical solution with the two invariance relations\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} S_h^k+I_h^k=N_h^0 \quad \text {and} \quad Y^k+N_b^k\le \kappa , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}we conclude that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (E^k,Y^k,S_{b1}^k,I_{b1}^k,S_{b2}^k,S_h^k,I_h^k)\in \Omega \qquad \text {for all } k\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Therefore, the set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega$$\end{document} is positively invariant under the first-order NSFD scheme. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Theorem 5

Assume that the numerical solution generated by the first-order NSFD scheme (11)–(17) is nonnegative for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k\ge 0$$\end{document}. Then the solution is bounded.

In particular,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_h^k=S_h^k+I_h^k=N_h^0, \qquad 0\le S_h^k,\ I_h^k\le N_h^0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}If the solution remains in the feasible region\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0\le Y^k+N_b^k\le \kappa , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0\le Y^k,\ S_{b1}^k,\ I_{b1}^k,\ S_{b2}^k\le \kappa . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Moreover,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0\le E^k\le M_E, \qquad M_E=\max \left\{ E^0,\frac{\lambda \kappa }{\tau +\delta _e}\right\} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hence, all state variables of the NSFD scheme remain bounded.

Proof

We first consider the host population. From Eqs. (16) and (17), we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{S_h^{k+1}-S_h^k}{\phi _6(\Delta t)} = \delta _d N_h^k - \beta _h S_h^{k+1}\frac{I_{b1}^{k-m_v}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{N_{b1}^k} - \delta _d S_h^{k+1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}and\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{I_h^{k+1}-I_h^k}{\phi _7(\Delta t)} = \beta _h S_h^k\frac{I_{b1}^{k-m_v}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{N_{b1}^k} - \delta _d I_h^{k+1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Since\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_h^k=S_h^k+I_h^k, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}adding the two host equations gives the evolution of the total host population. Using the recruitment term \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _dN_h^k$$\end{document} and the natural death terms \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$-\delta _dS_h^{k+1}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$-\delta _dI_h^{k+1}$$\end{document}, while the infection terms only transfer individuals between the susceptible and infected host classes, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_h^{k+1}=S_h^{k+1}+I_h^{k+1}=S_h^k+I_h^k=N_h^k. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hence,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_h^{k+1}=N_h^k \qquad \text {for all } k\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}By iteration,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_h^k=N_h^0 \qquad \text {for all } k\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Since the numerical solution is nonnegative, it follows immediately that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0\le S_h^k\le N_h^k=N_h^0, \qquad 0\le I_h^k\le N_h^k=N_h^0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Thus, both host compartments are bounded.

Next, consider the vector-related compartments. By assumption, the numerical solution remains in the biologically feasible region\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0\le Y^k+N_b^k\le \kappa , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_b^k=S_{b1}^k+I_{b1}^k+S_{b2}^k. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Since all quantities are nonnegative, we deduce that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0\le Y^k\le \kappa , \qquad 0\le N_b^k\le \kappa . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Because\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} S_{b1}^k\le N_b^k,\qquad I_{b1}^k\le N_b^k,\qquad S_{b2}^k\le N_b^k, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}it follows that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0\le S_{b1}^k\le \kappa ,\qquad 0\le I_{b1}^k\le \kappa ,\qquad 0\le S_{b2}^k\le \kappa . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Therefore, the adult vector compartments and the nymph compartment are bounded. It remains to show that the egg population is bounded.

From (11), we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{E^{k+1}-E^k}{\phi _1(\Delta t)} = \lambda N_b^k-(\tau +\delta _e)E^{k+1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Multiplying both sides by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _1(\Delta t)$$\end{document} gives\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1}-E^k = \phi _1(\Delta t)\lambda N_b^k - \phi _1(\Delta t)(\tau +\delta _e)E^{k+1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Rearranging terms, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1}\left[ 1+\phi _1(\Delta t)(\tau +\delta _e)\right] = E^k+\phi _1(\Delta t)\lambda N_b^k. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hence\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1} = \frac{E^k+\phi _1(\Delta t)\lambda N_b^k}{1+\phi _1(\Delta t)(\tau +\delta _e)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Since\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0\le N_b^k\le \kappa , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}we get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1} \le \frac{E^k+\phi _1(\Delta t)\lambda \kappa }{1+\phi _1(\Delta t)(\tau +\delta _e)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Now let\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} M_E=\max \left\{ E^0,\frac{\lambda \kappa }{\tau +\delta _e}\right\} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}We prove by induction that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^k\le M_E \qquad \text {for all } k\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k=0$$\end{document}, the result is true by the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_E$$\end{document}. Assume that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^k\le M_E. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1} \le \frac{M_E+\phi _1(\Delta t)\lambda \kappa }{1+\phi _1(\Delta t)(\tau +\delta _e)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Since\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} M_E\ge \frac{\lambda \kappa }{\tau +\delta _e}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lambda \kappa \le (\tau +\delta _e)M_E. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Therefore,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1} \le \frac{M_E+\phi _1(\Delta t)(\tau +\delta _e)M_E}{1+\phi _1(\Delta t)(\tau +\delta _e)} = M_E. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Thus,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1}\le M_E. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}By induction,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0\le E^k\le M_E \qquad \text {for all } k\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Combining all the above estimates, we conclude that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^k,\ Y^k,\ S_{b1}^k,\ I_{b1}^k,\ S_{b2}^k,\ S_h^k,\ I_h^k \end{aligned}$$\end{document}are all bounded for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k\ge 0$$\end{document}. Hence, the first-order NSFD scheme is bounded. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Remark on stability analysis of the delayed system. It is important to note that the stability analysis presented in this study is carried out for the discrete NSFD schemes rather than directly for the continuous delay differential system. In the delayed model, the transmission terms include factors of the form \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e^{-\delta _b \tau _h} and e^{-\delta _d \tau _v}$$\end{document}, which represent survival probabilities over the delay periods. These terms are independent of time and can therefore be treated as constant parameters within the numerical framework. Consequently, the proposed NSFD schemes incorporate the effect of delays through these biologically meaningful exponential terms, while preserving essential dynamical properties such as positivity and boundedness. The local stability analysis is thus performed on the resulting discrete dynamical systems, which serve as structure-preserving approximations of the original model.

Interpretation of stability results from an epidemiological perspective

In this section, we discuss the significance of the stability results derived in Theorems 5, 6, 9, and 10 in relation to the transmission dynamics of Chagas disease in dogs. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0<1$$\end{document}, the disease-free equilibrium is stable, indicating that the infection will eventually be eradicated from the community. On the other hand, when \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0>1$$\end{document}, the disease remains present in the community and converges to the endemic equilibrium.

Stability at the endemic equilibrium implies that the disease is permanently established and needs to be consistently managed. Moreover, global stability results imply that the disease dynamics does not depend on the initial state and only depends on the system’s parameters.

From this discussion, it can be noted that controlling disease transmission requires reduction of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0$$\end{document} to be less than 1.

Stability analysis of the first-order NSFD scheme

In this subsection, we study the local stability of the disease-free equilibrium and the endemic equilibrium of the first-order NSFD scheme by using the partial derivative method. Since infection dynamics is governed by the infected vector and infected host classes, it is sufficient to consider the discrete infected subsystem associated with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I_{b1}^k$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I_h^k$$\end{document}.

For the first-order NSFD scheme, the infected subsystem is given by18\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & I_{b1}^{k+1} = \frac{I_{b1}^k+\phi _4(\Delta t)\beta _v S_{b1}^k\dfrac{I_h^{k-m_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{N_h^k}}{1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}19\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & I_h^{k+1} = \frac{I_h^k+\phi _7(\Delta t)\beta _h S_h^k\dfrac{I_{b1}^{k-m_v}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{N_{b1}^k}}{1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Define the functions,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} F(I_{b1}^k,I_h^{k-m_h}) = \frac{I_{b1}^k+\phi _4(\Delta t)\beta _v S_{b1}^k\dfrac{I_h^{k-m_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{N_h^k}}{1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b}, \\ G(I_h^k,I_{b1}^{k-m_v}) = \frac{I_h^k+\phi _7(\Delta t)\beta _h S_h^k\dfrac{I_{b1}^{k-m_v}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{N_{b1}^k}}{1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}The stability conditions for the discrete system follow from the Jury stability criterion (see33).

Lemma 3

(Jury stability criterion) Let\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lambda ^2-\operatorname {tr}(J)\lambda +\det (J)=0 \end{aligned}$$\end{document}be the characteristic equation of a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2\times 2$$\end{document} discrete dynamical system with Jacobian matrix J. Then all eigenvalues of J lie inside the unit disk \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\lambda |<1$$\end{document} if and only if the following conditions hold:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\operatorname {tr}(J)+\det (J)>0, \\ 1+\operatorname {tr}(J)+\det (J)>0, \\ 1-\det (J)>0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Theorem 6

(Local stability of the disease-free equilibrium) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_0=(E^0,Y^0,S_{b1}^0,0,S_{b2}^0,N_h,0)$$\end{document} be the disease-free equilibrium of the first-order NSFD scheme. Then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_0$$\end{document} is locally asymptotically stable if the eigenvalues of the Jacobian matrix\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} J(E_0)= \begin{pmatrix} \dfrac{\partial F}{\partial I_{b1}^k} & \dfrac{\partial F}{\partial I_h^{k-m_h}} \\ \dfrac{\partial G}{\partial I_{b1}^{k-m_v}} & \dfrac{\partial G}{\partial I_h^k} \end{pmatrix}_{E_0} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lie inside the unit disk. Equivalently, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_0$$\end{document} is locally asymptotically stable if the Jury conditions\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\operatorname {tr}(J(E_0))+\det (J(E_0))>0, \\ 1+\operatorname {tr}(J(E_0))+\det (J(E_0))>0, \\ 1-\det (J(E_0))>0 \end{aligned}$$\end{document}are satisfied. In particular, this occurs when \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0<1$$\end{document}. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0>1$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_0$$\end{document} is unstable.

Proof

At the disease-free equilibrium,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} S_{b1}^k=S_{b1}^0,\qquad S_h^k=N_h,\qquad N_h^k=N_h,\qquad N_{b1}^k=S_{b1}^0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hence (18) and (19) become\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} I_{b1}^{k+1} = \frac{I_{b1}^k+\phi _4(\Delta t)\beta _v\dfrac{S_{b1}^0}{N_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}I_h^{k-m_h}}{1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b}, \\ I_h^{k+1} = \frac{I_h^k+\phi _7(\Delta t)\beta _h\dfrac{N_h}{S_{b1}^0}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}I_{b1}^{k-m_v}}{1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Now we compute the required partial derivatives. First,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{\partial F}{\partial I_{b1}^k}\Bigg |_{E_0} = \frac{1}{1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b}, \qquad \frac{\partial F}{\partial I_h^{k-m_h}}\Bigg |_{E_0} = \frac{\phi _4(\Delta t)\beta _v\dfrac{S_{b1}^0}{N_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Similarly,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{\partial G}{\partial I_{b1}^{k-m_v}}\Bigg |_{E_0} = \frac{\phi _7(\Delta t)\beta _h\dfrac{N_h}{S_{b1}^0}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d}, \qquad \frac{\partial G}{\partial I_h^k}\Bigg |_{E_0} = \frac{1}{1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Therefore, the Jacobian matrix at the disease-free equilibrium is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} J(E_0)= \begin{pmatrix} \dfrac{1}{1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b} & \dfrac{\phi _4(\Delta t)\beta _v\dfrac{S_{b1}^0}{N_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b} \\ \dfrac{\phi _7(\Delta t)\beta _h\dfrac{N_h}{S_{b1}^0}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d} & \dfrac{1}{1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d} \end{pmatrix}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Its trace is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \operatorname {tr}(J(E_0)) = \frac{1}{1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b} + \frac{1}{1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}and its determinant is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \det (J(E_0)) = \frac{1}{(1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b)(1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d)} \left[ 1-\phi _4(\Delta t)\phi _7(\Delta t)\beta _v\beta _h e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)} \right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}The characteristic polynomial of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$J(E_0)$$\end{document} is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lambda ^2-\operatorname {tr}(J(E_0))\lambda +\det (J(E_0))=0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}To verify the first Jury condition (see Lamma 3), we compute\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\operatorname {tr}(J(E_0))+\det (J(E_0)) = \frac{\phi _4(\Delta t)\phi _7(\Delta t)}{(1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b)(1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d)} \left[ \delta _b\delta _d- \beta _v\beta _h e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)} \right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hence,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\operatorname {tr}(J(E_0))+\det (J(E_0))>0 \end{aligned}$$\end{document}whenever\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \delta _b\delta _d> \beta _v\beta _h e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}For the second condition,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1+\operatorname {tr}(J(E_0))+\det (J(E_0))>0, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}which clearly holds since all involved parameters are positive and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1+\phi _i(\Delta t)\delta _j>0$$\end{document}.

Finally, for the third condition,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\det (J(E_0)) = \frac{AB-1}{AB} + \frac{\phi _4(\Delta t)\phi _7(\Delta t)\beta _v\beta _h e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)}}{AB}>0, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} A=1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b, \qquad B=1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A>1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B>1$$\end{document}, it follows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$AB>1$$\end{document}, and therefore \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1-\det (J(E_0))>0$$\end{document}.

Thus, all Jury stability conditions are satisfied, provided\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {\mathcal {R}}_0^2= \frac{\beta _v\beta _h e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)}}{\delta _b\delta _d}<1. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hence, if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0<1$$\end{document}, the off-diagonal infection feedback is sufficiently weak and the above Jury conditions hold. Therefore, all eigenvalues of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$J(E_0)$$\end{document} lie inside the unit circle, and the disease-free equilibrium is locally asymptotically stable. On the other hand, if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0>1$$\end{document}, then the transmission of infection dominates the damping terms, so that at least one eigenvalue crosses the unit circle. Thus, the disease-free equilibrium becomes unstable. This completes the proof. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Theorem 7

(Local stability of the endemic equilibrium) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^*=(E^*,Y^*,S_{b1}^*,I_{b1}^*,S_{b2}^*,S_h^*,I_h^*)$$\end{document} be the endemic equilibrium of the first-order NSFD scheme, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I_{b1}^*>0$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I_h^*>0$$\end{document}. Then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^*$$\end{document} is locally asymptotically stable if the eigenvalues of the Jacobian matrix\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} J(E^*)= \begin{pmatrix} \dfrac{\partial F}{\partial I_{b1}^k} & \dfrac{\partial F}{\partial I_h^{k-m_h}} \\ \dfrac{\partial G}{\partial I_{b1}^{k-m_v}} & \dfrac{\partial G}{\partial I_h^k} \end{pmatrix}_{E^*} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lie inside the unit disk. Equivalently, the endemic equilibrium is locally asymptotically stable if\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\operatorname {tr}(J(E^*))+\det (J(E^*))>0, \\ 1+\operatorname {tr}(J(E^*))+\det (J(E^*))>0, \\ 1-\det (J(E^*))>0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Proof
