At the endemic equilibrium,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} S_{b1}^k=S_{b1}^*,\qquad I_{b1}^k=I_{b1}^*,\qquad S_h^k=S_h^*,\qquad I_h^k=I_h^*, \qquad N_h^k=N_h,\qquad N_{b1}^k=N_{b1}^*=S_{b1}^*+I_{b1}^*. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Thus, the infected subsystem becomes\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} I_{b1}^{k+1} = \frac{I_{b1}^k+\phi _4(\Delta t)\beta _v\dfrac{S_{b1}^*}{N_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}I_h^{k-m_h}}{1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b}, \qquad I_h^{k+1} = \frac{I_h^k+\phi _7(\Delta t)\beta _h\dfrac{S_h^*}{N_{b1}^*}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}I_{b1}^{k-m_v}}{1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}We now compute the Jacobian entries at \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^*$$\end{document}. First,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{\partial F}{\partial I_{b1}^k}\Bigg |_{E^*} = \frac{1}{1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b}, \qquad \frac{\partial F}{\partial I_h^{k-m_h}}\Bigg |_{E^*} = \frac{\phi _4(\Delta t)\beta _v\dfrac{S_{b1}^*}{N_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Similarly,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{\partial G}{\partial I_{b1}^{k-m_v}}\Bigg |_{E^*} = \frac{\phi _7(\Delta t)\beta _h\dfrac{S_h^*}{N_{b1}^*}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d}, \qquad \frac{\partial G}{\partial I_h^k}\Bigg |_{E^*} = \frac{1}{1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hence,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} J(E^*)= \begin{pmatrix} \dfrac{1}{1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b} & \dfrac{\phi _4(\Delta t)\beta _v\dfrac{S_{b1}^*}{N_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b} \\ \dfrac{\phi _7(\Delta t)\beta _h\dfrac{S_h^*}{N_{b1}^*}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d} & \dfrac{1}{1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d} \end{pmatrix}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Its trace is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \operatorname {tr}(J(E^*)) = \frac{1}{1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b} + \frac{1}{1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}while its determinant is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \det (J(E^*)) = \frac{1}{(1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b)(1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d)} \left[ 1-\phi _4(\Delta t)\phi _7(\Delta t)\beta _v\beta _h \frac{S_{b1}^*S_h^*}{N_hN_{b1}^*} e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)} \right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Therefore, the characteristic polynomial is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lambda ^2-\operatorname {tr}(J(E^*))\lambda +\det (J(E^*))=0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}By the Jury stability criterion, the endemic equilibrium \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^*$$\end{document} is locally asymptotically stable if and only if\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\operatorname {tr}(J(E^*))+\det (J(E^*))>0, \\ 1+\operatorname {tr}(J(E^*))+\det (J(E^*))>0, \\ 1-\det (J(E^*))>0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}To verify the Jury stability conditions for the endemic equilibrium \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^*$$\end{document}, let\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} A=1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b, \qquad B=1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Then the trace and determinant of the Jacobian matrix are\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \operatorname {tr}(J(E^*))=\frac{1}{A}+\frac{1}{B}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \det (J(E^*))= \frac{1}{AB} \left[ 1-\phi _4(\Delta t)\phi _7(\Delta t)\beta _v\beta _h \frac{S_{b1}^*S_h^*}{N_hN_{b1}^*} e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)} \right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}First condition.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\operatorname {tr}(J(E^*))+\det (J(E^*)) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Substituting the expressions of trace and determinant gives\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\left( \frac{1}{A}+\frac{1}{B}\right) + \frac{1}{AB} \left[ 1-\phi _4(\Delta t)\phi _7(\Delta t)\beta _v\beta _h \frac{S_{b1}^*S_h^*}{N_hN_{b1}^*} e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)} \right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}After simplification, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\operatorname {tr}(J(E^*))+\det (J(E^*)) = \frac{(A-1)(B-1)}{AB} - \frac{\phi _4(\Delta t)\phi _7(\Delta t)\beta _v\beta _h \frac{S_{b1}^*S_h^*}{N_hN_{b1}^*} e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)}}{AB}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Since\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} A-1=\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b, \qquad B-1=\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\operatorname {tr}(J(E^*))+\det (J(E^*)) = \frac{\phi _4(\Delta t)\phi _7(\Delta t)}{(1+\phi _4(\Delta t)\delta _b)(1+\phi _7(\Delta t)\delta _d)} \left[ \delta _b\delta _d - \beta _v\beta _h \frac{S_{b1}^*S_h^*}{N_hN_{b1}^*} e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)} \right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hence,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\operatorname {tr}(J(E^*))+\det (J(E^*))>0 \end{aligned}$$\end{document}whenever\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \delta _b\delta _d > \beta _v\beta _h \frac{S_{b1}^*S_h^*}{N_hN_{b1}^*} e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Second condition.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1+\operatorname {tr}(J(E^*))+\det (J(E^*)) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Substituting the trace and determinant yields\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1+\frac{1}{A}+\frac{1}{B} + \frac{1}{AB} \left[ 1-\phi _4(\Delta t)\phi _7(\Delta t)\beta _v\beta _h \frac{S_{b1}^*S_h^*}{N_hN_{b1}^*} e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)} \right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A>1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B>1$$\end{document}, it follows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{A}>0$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{B}>0$$\end{document}. Moreover, the determinant term is bounded; therefore\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1+\operatorname {tr}(J(E^*))+\det (J(E^*))>0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Third condition.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\det (J(E^*)) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Substituting the determinant gives\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1- \frac{1}{AB} \left[ 1-\phi _4(\Delta t)\phi _7(\Delta t)\beta _v\beta _h \frac{S_{b1}^*S_h^*}{N_hN_{b1}^*} e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)} \right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Rewriting,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\det (J(E^*)) = \frac{AB-1}{AB} + \frac{\phi _4(\Delta t)\phi _7(\Delta t)\beta _v\beta _h \frac{S_{b1}^*S_h^*}{N_hN_{b1}^*} e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)}}{AB}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A>1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B>1$$\end{document}, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$AB>1$$\end{document}, and therefore\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\det (J(E^*))>0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Thus, all Jury stability conditions are satisfied, implying that the endemic equilibrium \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^*$$\end{document} is locally asymptotically stable. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Formulation of the second-order NSFD method

In this subsection, we construct a second-order nonstandard finite difference (NSFD) scheme for the delayed canine Chagas disease model. The construction of the second-order framework proposed in30. The main objective of this discretization is to improve the numerical accuracy of the first-order scheme while preserving the essential qualitative properties of the continuous model, such as positivity, boundedness of solutions, and dynamical consistency.

Following the second-order NSFD philosophy, the classical denominator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta t$$\end{document} is replaced by state-dependent denominator functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _i(\Delta t,\cdot )$$\end{document} satisfying the consistency condition\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \phi _i(\Delta t,\cdot ) = \Delta t + {\mathcal {O}}(\Delta t^2), \qquad \Delta t \rightarrow 0, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}while additional weighting parameters \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta _i$$\end{document} are introduced in order to achieve second-order accuracy30. Using these denominator functions and nonlocal approximations of the nonlinear incidence terms, the second-order NSFD scheme for the delayed canine Chagas disease model takes the form20\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{E^{k+1}-E^k}{\phi _1(\Delta t,E^k,Y^k)} = \lambda N_b^k - (\tau +\delta _e)E^{k+1} + \theta _1 E^k - \theta _1 E^{k+1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}21\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{Y^{k+1}-Y^k}{\phi _2(\Delta t,E^k,Y^k)} = \tau E^k \left( 1-\frac{Y^k+N_b^k}{\kappa }\right) - (\gamma _y+\delta _y)Y^{k+1} + \theta _2 Y^k - \theta _2 Y^{k+1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}22\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{S_{b1}^{k+1}-S_{b1}^k}{\phi _3(\Delta t,S_{b1}^k,I_h^k)} = \varphi \gamma _y Y^k - \beta _v S_{b1}^{k+1} \frac{I_h^{k-m_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{N_h^k} - \delta _b S_{b1}^{k+1} + \theta _3 S_{b1}^k - \theta _3 S_{b1}^{k+1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}23\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{I_{b1}^{k+1}-I_{b1}^k}{\phi _4(\Delta t,I_{b1}^k,I_h^k)} = \beta _v S_{b1}^k \frac{I_h^{k-m_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{N_h^k} - \delta _b I_{b1}^{k+1} + \theta _4 I_{b1}^k - \theta _4 I_{b1}^{k+1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}24\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{S_{b2}^{k+1}-S_{b2}^k}{\phi _5(\Delta t,S_{b2}^k,Y^k)} = (1-\varphi )\gamma _y Y^k - \delta _b S_{b2}^{k+1} + \theta _5 S_{b2}^k - \theta _5 S_{b2}^{k+1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}25\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{S_h^{k+1}-S_h^k}{\phi _6(\Delta t,S_h^k,I_{b1}^k)} = \delta _d N_h^k - \beta _h S_h^{k+1} \frac{I_{b1}^{k-m_v}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{N_{b1}^k} - \delta _d S_h^{k+1} + \theta _6 S_h^k - \theta _6 S_h^{k+1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}26\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{I_h^{k+1}-I_h^k}{\phi _7(\Delta t,I_h^k,I_{b1}^k)} = \beta _h S_h^k \frac{I_{b1}^{k-m_v}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{N_{b1}^k} - \delta _d I_h^{k+1} + \theta _7 I_h^k - \theta _7 I_h^{k+1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}The above system represents the second-order NSFD approximation of the delayed canine Chagas disease model. The use of state-dependent denominator functions together with nonlocal discretization ensures that the numerical scheme preserves the main qualitative properties of the continuous system while improving the accuracy of the approximation29,30.

Qualitative properties of the second-order NSFD scheme

In this subsection, we prove the positivity and boundedness of solutions generated by the second-order NSFD scheme. For simplicity, in some instances, we omit the arguments of the denominator functions.

Theorem 8

The positivity of the delayed canine Chagas disease model is preserved for any given step size by the second-order NSFD scheme whenever \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta _i\ge 0$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i=1,\dots ,7$$\end{document}.

Proof

The second-order NSFD scheme can be rewritten in the following explicit form:27\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & E^{k+1} = \frac{E^k+\phi _1\big (\lambda N_b^k+\theta _1E^k\big )}{1+\phi _1(\tau +\delta _e+\theta _1)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}28\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & Y^{k+1} = \frac{Y^k+\phi _2\left[ \tau E^k\left( 1-\dfrac{Y^k+N_b^k}{\kappa }\right) +\theta _2Y^k\right] }{1+\phi _2(\gamma _y+\delta _y+\theta _2)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}29\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & S_{b1}^{k+1} = \frac{S_{b1}^k+\phi _3\big (\varphi \gamma _yY^k+\theta _3S_{b1}^k\big )}{1+\phi _3\left( \beta _v\dfrac{I_h^{k-m_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{N_h^k}+\delta _b+\theta _3\right) }, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}30\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & I_{b1}^{k+1} = \frac{I_{b1}^k+\phi _4\left( \beta _vS_{b1}^k\dfrac{I_h^{k-m_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{N_h^k}+\theta _4I_{b1}^k\right) }{1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}31\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & S_{b2}^{k+1} = \frac{S_{b2}^k+\phi _5\big ((1-\varphi )\gamma _yY^k+\theta _5S_{b2}^k\big )}{1+\phi _5(\delta _b+\theta _5)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}32\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & S_h^{k+1} = \frac{S_h^k+\phi _6\big (\delta _dN_h^k+\theta _6S_h^k\big )}{1+\phi _6\left( \beta _h\dfrac{I_{b1}^{k-m_v}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{N_{b1}^k}+\delta _d+\theta _6\right) }, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}33\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & I_h^{k+1} = \frac{I_h^k+\phi _7\left( \beta _hS_h^k\dfrac{I_{b1}^{k-m_v}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{N_{b1}^k}+\theta _7I_h^k\right) }{1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Since all parameters of the system are nonnegative, the denominator functions satisfy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _i>0$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta _i\ge 0$$\end{document}, it follows from (27)–(33) that if\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^k,\;Y^k,\;S_{b1}^k,\;I_{b1}^k,\;S_{b2}^k,\;S_h^k,\;I_h^k\ge 0, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1},\;Y^{k+1},\;S_{b1}^{k+1},\;I_{b1}^{k+1},\;S_{b2}^{k+1},\;S_h^{k+1},\;I_h^{k+1}\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Indeed, each numerator in (27)–(33) is a sum of nonnegative terms, while each denominator is strictly positive. Hence, the positivity holds unconditionally for all state variables and for any step size. This completes the proof. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Theorem 9

The second-order NSFD scheme defines a discrete dynamical system on the set\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} D= \left\{ (E^k,Y^k,S_{b1}^k,I_{b1}^k,S_{b2}^k,S_h^k,I_h^k): 0\le S_h^k+I_h^k\le N_h,\; 0\le Y^k+N_b^k\le \kappa ,\; 0\le E^k\le M_E \right\} , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_b^k=S_{b1}^k+I_{b1}^k+S_{b2}^k, \qquad M_E=\max \left\{ E^0,\frac{\lambda \kappa }{\tau +\delta _e}\right\} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}In particular, all solutions of the second-order NSFD scheme remain bounded.

Proof

Let\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_h^k=S_h^k+I_h^k \end{aligned}$$\end{document}denote the total host population. Using (32) and (33), we write\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} S_h^{k+1} = \frac{S_h^k+\phi _6\big (\delta _dN_h^k+\theta _6S_h^k\big )}{1+\phi _6\left( \beta _h\dfrac{I_{b1}^{k-m_v}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{N_{b1}^k}+\delta _d+\theta _6\right) }, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}and\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} I_h^{k+1} = \frac{I_h^k+\phi _7\left( \beta _hS_h^k\dfrac{I_{b1}^{k-m_v}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{N_{b1}^k}+\theta _7I_h^k\right) }{1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Adding the host equations in their implicit form gives\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{S_h^{k+1}-S_h^k}{\phi _6} + \frac{I_h^{k+1}-I_h^k}{\phi _7} = \delta _dN_h^k-\delta _dS_h^{k+1}-\delta _dI_h^{k+1} +\theta _6S_h^k-\theta _6S_h^{k+1} +\theta _7I_h^k-\theta _7I_h^{k+1} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}after cancelation of the infection terms. In the particular case where the same denominator function and weight are used in the host equations, that is, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _6=\phi _7=\phi _h$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta _6=\theta _7=\theta _h$$\end{document}, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{N_h^{k+1}-N_h^k}{\phi _h} = \delta _dN_h^k-\delta _dN_h^{k+1} +\theta _hN_h^k-\theta _hN_h^{k+1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hence,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_h^{k+1}\big [1+\phi _h(\delta _d+\theta _h)\big ] = N_h^k\big [1+\phi _h(\delta _d+\theta _h)\big ], \end{aligned}$$\end{document}which implies\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_h^{k+1}=N_h^k. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Therefore,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_h^k=N_h^0=N_h \qquad \text {for all } k\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Since the solution is nonnegative, it follows that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0\le S_h^k\le N_h,\qquad 0\le I_h^k\le N_h \qquad \text {for all } k\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Next, consider the vector-related quantity\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} W^k=Y^k+N_b^k = Y^k+S_{b1}^k+I_{b1}^k+S_{b2}^k. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}The nymph equation contains the logistic factor\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\frac{Y^k+N_b^k}{\kappa } = 1-\frac{W^k}{\kappa }. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W^k=\kappa$$\end{document}, then the recruitment contribution in the nymph equation vanishes. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W^k>\kappa$$\end{document}, then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\frac{W^k}{\kappa }<0, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}so that the recruitment term becomes nonpositive, while all mortality terms remain dissipative. Hence, the dynamics force the solution back into the region\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} W^k\le \kappa . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Therefore, for initial data satisfying\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0\le Y^k+N_b^k\le \kappa , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0\le Y^k+N_b^k\le \kappa \qquad \text {for all } k\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Consequently,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0\le Y^k\le \kappa , \qquad 0\le N_b^k\le \kappa , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}and therefore\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0\le S_{b1}^k,\ I_{b1}^k,\ S_{b2}^k\le \kappa \qquad \text {for all } k\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}It remains to show that the egg population is bounded. From (27), we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1} = \frac{E^k+\phi _1\big (\lambda N_b^k+\theta _1E^k\big )}{1+\phi _1(\tau +\delta _e+\theta _1)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le N_b^k\le \kappa$$\end{document}, it follows that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1} \le \frac{E^k+\phi _1(\lambda \kappa +\theta _1E^k)}{1+\phi _1(\tau +\delta _e+\theta _1)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Thus,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1} \le \frac{(1+\phi _1\theta _1)E^k+\phi _1\lambda \kappa }{1+\phi _1(\tau +\delta _e+\theta _1)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Let\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} M_E=\max \left\{ E^0,\frac{\lambda \kappa }{\tau +\delta _e}\right\} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}We prove by induction that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^k\le M_E$$\end{document} for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k\ge 0$$\end{document}. The claim is true for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k=0$$\end{document}. Assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^k\le M_E$$\end{document}. Then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1} \le \frac{(1+\phi _1\theta _1)M_E+\phi _1\lambda \kappa }{1+\phi _1(\tau +\delta _e+\theta _1)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Since\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} M_E\ge \frac{\lambda \kappa }{\tau +\delta _e}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lambda \kappa \le (\tau +\delta _e)M_E. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hence,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E^{k+1} \le \frac{(1+\phi _1\theta _1)M_E+\phi _1(\tau +\delta _e)M_E}{1+\phi _1(\tau +\delta _e+\theta _1)} = M_E. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Therefore,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0\le E^k\le M_E \qquad \text {for all } k\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Combining the above estimates, we conclude that all state variables remain in the set D, and therefore the second-order NSFD scheme is bounded. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Stability analysis of the second-order NSFD scheme

In this subsection, we investigate the local stability of the disease-free equilibrium and the endemic equilibrium of the second-order NSFD scheme by using the partial derivative method. For this purpose, we consider the discrete infected subsystem associated with infected adult vectors feeding on dogs and infected dogs.

For the second-order NSFD scheme, the infected subsystem is given by34\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & I_{b1}^{k+1} = \frac{(1+\phi _4\theta _4)I_{b1}^k+\phi _4\beta _v S_{b1}^k\dfrac{I_h^{k-m_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{N_h^k}}{1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}35\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & I_h^{k+1} = \frac{(1+\phi _7\theta _7)I_h^k+\phi _7\beta _h S_h^k\dfrac{I_{b1}^{k-m_v}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{N_{b1}^k}}{1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where, for simplicity, we write\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \phi _4=\phi _4(\Delta t,I_{b1}^k,I_h^k), \qquad \phi _7=\phi _7(\Delta t,I_h^k,I_{b1}^k). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Define\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} F(I_{b1}^k,I_h^{k-m_h}) = \frac{(1+\phi _4\theta _4)I_{b1}^k+\phi _4\beta _v S_{b1}^k\dfrac{I_h^{k-m_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{N_h^k}}{1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}and\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} G(I_h^k,I_{b1}^{k-m_v}) = \frac{(1+\phi _7\theta _7)I_h^k+\phi _7\beta _h S_h^k\dfrac{I_{b1}^{k-m_v}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{N_{b1}^k}}{1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Theorem 10

(Local stability of the disease-free equilibrium) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_0=(E^0,Y^0,S_{b1}^0,0,S_{b2}^0,N_h,0)$$\end{document} be the disease-free equilibrium of the second-order NSFD scheme. Then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_0$$\end{document} is locally asymptotically stable if the eigenvalues of the Jacobian matrix\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} J(E_0)= \begin{pmatrix} \dfrac{\partial F}{\partial I_{b1}^k} & \dfrac{\partial F}{\partial I_h^{k-m_h}} \\ \dfrac{\partial G}{\partial I_{b1}^{k-m_v}} & \dfrac{\partial G}{\partial I_h^k} \end{pmatrix}_{E_0} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lie inside the unit disk. Equivalently, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_0$$\end{document} is locally asymptotically stable if the Jury conditions\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\operatorname {tr}(J(E_0))+\det (J(E_0))>0, \\ 1+\operatorname {tr}(J(E_0))+\det (J(E_0))>0, \\ 1-\det (J(E_0))>0 \end{aligned}$$\end{document}are satisfied. In particular, this holds when \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0<1$$\end{document}. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0>1$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_0$$\end{document} is unstable.

Proof

At the disease-free equilibrium, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} S_{b1}^k=S_{b1}^0,\qquad S_h^k=N_h,\qquad N_h^k=N_h,\qquad N_{b1}^k=S_{b1}^0, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}together with\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} I_{b1}^k=0,\qquad I_h^k=0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hence, Eqs. (34) and (35) reduce to\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} I_{b1}^{k+1} = \frac{(1+\phi _4\theta _4)I_{b1}^k+\phi _4\beta _v\dfrac{S_{b1}^0}{N_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}I_h^{k-m_h}}{1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}and\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} I_h^{k+1} = \frac{(1+\phi _7\theta _7)I_h^k+\phi _7\beta _h\dfrac{N_h}{S_{b1}^0}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}I_{b1}^{k-m_v}}{1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Now we compute the partial derivatives of the discrete map at \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_0$$\end{document}. First,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{\partial F}{\partial I_{b1}^k}\Bigg |_{E_0} = \frac{1+\phi _4\theta _4}{1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}because the transmission term in F depends on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I_h^{k-m_h}$$\end{document} and not on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I_{b1}^k$$\end{document}. Next,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{\partial F}{\partial I_h^{k-m_h}}\Bigg |_{E_0} = \frac{\phi _4\beta _v\dfrac{S_{b1}^0}{N_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Similarly,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{\partial G}{\partial I_{b1}^{k-m_v}}\Bigg |_{E_0} = \frac{\phi _7\beta _h\dfrac{N_h}{S_{b1}^0}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)}, \qquad \frac{\partial G}{\partial I_h^k}\Bigg |_{E_0} = \frac{1+\phi _7\theta _7}{1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Therefore, the Jacobian matrix at the disease-free equilibrium is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} J(E_0)= \begin{pmatrix} \dfrac{1+\phi _4\theta _4}{1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)} & \dfrac{\phi _4\beta _v\dfrac{S_{b1}^0}{N_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)} \\ \dfrac{\phi _7\beta _h\dfrac{N_h}{S_{b1}^0}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)} & \dfrac{1+\phi _7\theta _7}{1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)} \end{pmatrix}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Its trace is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \operatorname {tr}(J(E_0)) = \frac{1+\phi _4\theta _4}{1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)} + \frac{1+\phi _7\theta _7}{1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}while its determinant is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \det (J(E_0)) = \frac{(1+\phi _4\theta _4)(1+\phi _7\theta _7)}{[1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)][1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)]} - \frac{\phi _4\phi _7\beta _v\beta _h e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)}}{[1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)][1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)]}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Thus, the characteristic polynomial of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$J(E_0)$$\end{document} is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lambda ^2-\operatorname {tr}(J(E_0))\lambda +\det (J(E_0))=0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}By the Jury criterion for a two-dimensional discrete system, the disease-free equilibrium is locally asymptotically stable if and only if\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\operatorname {tr}(J(E_0))+\det (J(E_0))>0, \\ 1+\operatorname {tr}(J(E_0))+\det (J(E_0))>0, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}and\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\det (J(E_0))>0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}To verify the Jury stability conditions for the disease-free equilibrium \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_0$$\end{document}, let\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} A=1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4), \qquad B=1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Then the trace and determinant can be written as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \operatorname {tr}(J(E_0)) = \frac{1+\phi _4\theta _4}{A} + \frac{1+\phi _7\theta _7}{B}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \det (J(E_0)) = \frac{(1+\phi _4\theta _4)(1+\phi _7\theta _7)}{AB} - \frac{\phi _4\phi _7\beta _v\beta _h e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)}}{AB}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}First condition.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\operatorname {tr}(J(E_0))+\det (J(E_0)). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Substituting the expressions of the trace and determinant gives\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1- \left( \frac{1+\phi _4\theta _4}{A} + \frac{1+\phi _7\theta _7}{B} \right) + \frac{(1+\phi _4\theta _4)(1+\phi _7\theta _7)}{AB} - \frac{\phi _4\phi _7\beta _v\beta _h e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)}}{AB}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}After arranging the common denominator AB, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\operatorname {tr}(J(E_0))+\det (J(E_0)) = \frac{\phi _4\phi _7}{AB} \left[ \delta _b\delta _d- \beta _v\beta _h e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)} \right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hence\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\operatorname {tr}(J(E_0))+\det (J(E_0))>0 \end{aligned}$$\end{document}whenever\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \delta _b\delta _d> \beta _v\beta _h e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Second condition.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1+\operatorname {tr}(J(E_0))+\det (J(E_0)). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Substituting the trace and determinant yields\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1+ \frac{1+\phi _4\theta _4}{A} + \frac{1+\phi _7\theta _7}{B} + \frac{(1+\phi _4\theta _4)(1+\phi _7\theta _7)}{AB} - \frac{\phi _4\phi _7\beta _v\beta _h e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)}}{AB}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Since all parameters are positive and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A>1$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B>1$$\end{document}, the positive terms dominate, and therefore\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1+\operatorname {tr}(J(E_0))+\det (J(E_0))>0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Third condition.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\det (J(E_0)). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Substituting the determinant gives\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1- \frac{(1+\phi _4\theta _4)(1+\phi _7\theta _7)}{AB} + \frac{\phi _4\phi _7\beta _v\beta _h e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)}}{AB}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Rewriting,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\det (J(E_0)) = \frac{AB-(1+\phi _4\theta _4)(1+\phi _7\theta _7)}{AB} + \frac{\phi _4\phi _7\beta _v\beta _h e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)}}{AB}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Since\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} AB-(1+\phi _4\theta _4)(1+\phi _7\theta _7) = \phi _4\phi _7\delta _b\delta _d>0, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}it follows that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\det (J(E_0))>0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Thus all Jury stability conditions are satisfied whenever \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0^2= \frac{\beta _v\beta _h}{\delta _b\delta _d} e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)}<1.$$\end{document} Therefore the disease-free equilibrium \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_0$$\end{document} is locally asymptotically stable. In contrast, if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0>1$$\end{document}, at least one eigenvalue crosses the unit circle and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_0$$\end{document} becomes unstable. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Theorem 11

(Local stability of the endemic equilibrium) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^*=(E^*,Y^*,S_{b1}^*,I_{b1}^*,S_{b2}^*,S_h^*,I_h^*)$$\end{document} be the endemic equilibrium of the second-order NSFD scheme, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I_{b1}^*>0, I_h^*>0$$\end{document}. Then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^*$$\end{document} is locally asymptotically stable if the eigenvalues of the Jacobian matrix\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} J(E^*)= \begin{pmatrix} \dfrac{\partial F}{\partial I_{b1}^k} & \dfrac{\partial F}{\partial I_h^{k-m_h}} \\ \dfrac{\partial G}{\partial I_{b1}^{k-m_v}} & \dfrac{\partial G}{\partial I_h^k} \end{pmatrix}_{E^*} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lie inside the unit disk. Equivalently, the endemic equilibrium is locally asymptotically stable if\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 1-\operatorname {tr}(J(E^*))+\det (J(E^*))>0, \\ 1+\operatorname {tr}(J(E^*))+\det (J(E^*))>0, \\ 1-\det (J(E^*))>0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Proof

At the endemic equilibrium,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} S_{b1}^k=S_{b1}^*,\qquad I_{b1}^k=I_{b1}^*,\qquad S_h^k=S_h^*,\qquad I_h^k=I_h^*, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}and\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_h^k=N_h,\qquad N_{b1}^k=N_{b1}^*=S_{b1}^*+I_{b1}^*. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hence, the infected subsystem (34)–(35) becomes\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} I_{b1}^{k+1} = \frac{(1+\phi _4\theta _4)I_{b1}^k+\phi _4\beta _v\dfrac{S_{b1}^*}{N_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}I_h^{k-m_h}}{1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)}, \qquad I_h^{k+1} = \frac{(1+\phi _7\theta _7)I_h^k+\phi _7\beta _h\dfrac{S_h^*}{N_{b1}^*}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}I_{b1}^{k-m_v}}{1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}We now compute the Jacobian entries at \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E^*$$\end{document}. First,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{\partial F}{\partial I_{b1}^k}\Bigg |_{E^*} = \frac{1+\phi _4\theta _4}{1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)}, \qquad \frac{\partial F}{\partial I_h^{k-m_h}}\Bigg |_{E^*} = \frac{\phi _4\beta _v\dfrac{S_{b1}^*}{N_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Similarly,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{\partial G}{\partial I_{b1}^{k-m_v}}\Bigg |_{E^*} = \frac{\phi _7\beta _h\dfrac{S_h^*}{N_{b1}^*}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)}, \qquad \frac{\partial G}{\partial I_h^k}\Bigg |_{E^*} = \frac{1+\phi _7\theta _7}{1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Therefore, the Jacobian matrix at the endemic equilibrium is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} J(E^*)= \begin{pmatrix} \dfrac{1+\phi _4\theta _4}{1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)} & \dfrac{\phi _4\beta _v\dfrac{S_{b1}^*}{N_h}e^{-\delta _b\tau _h}}{1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)} \\ \dfrac{\phi _7\beta _h\dfrac{S_h^*}{N_{b1}^*}e^{-\delta _d\tau _v}}{1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)} & \dfrac{1+\phi _7\theta _7}{1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)} \end{pmatrix}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Its trace is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \operatorname {tr}(J(E^*)) = \frac{1+\phi _4\theta _4}{1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)} + \frac{1+\phi _7\theta _7}{1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}and its determinant is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \det (J(E^*)) = \frac{(1+\phi _4\theta _4)(1+\phi _7\theta _7)}{[1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)][1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)]} - \frac{\phi _4\phi _7\beta _v\beta _h\dfrac{S_{b1}^*S_h^*}{N_hN_{b1}^*}e^{-(\delta _b\tau _h+\delta _d\tau _v)}}{[1+\phi _4(\delta _b+\theta _4)][1+\phi _7(\delta _d+\theta _7)]}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Thus, the characteristic polynomial of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$J(E^*)$$\end{document} is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lambda ^2-\operatorname {tr}(J(E^*))\lambda +\det (J(E^*))=0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}To verify the Jury stability conditions for the endemic equilibrium \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} _7). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Then the trace and determinant can be written as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} is locally asymptotically stable. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} Epidemiological data and global prevalence in dogs

To ensure biological realism and reliability of the numerical investigations, epidemiological information related to Chagas disease was compiled from international public health reports and published veterinary studies. According to the World Health Organization (WHO), Chagas disease, caused by the protozoan parasite Trypanosoma cruzi, affects approximately 6–7 million people worldwide. Each year nearly \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$30\,000$$\end{document} new infections are reported, while the disease is responsible for roughly \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$10\,000$$\end{document} deaths annually. More than 70 million individuals remain at risk of infection, primarily in endemic regions of Latin America, although population mobility has contributed to its emergence in non-endemic regions such as North America and Europe34,35. Within the domestic transmission cycle, dogs have been widely recognized as important reservoir hosts of T. cruzi. Many epidemiological studies have demonstrated that diseased dogs play a major role in the sustainability of parasite cycling between the mammalian hosts and triatomine vectors in endemic communities36. Canine populations have a significant role in the epidemiology of the disease because of their close association with human settlements, and due to their daily contact with these vectors of infection. There have been serological surveys that were held in various endemic nations and the report was that there was a big percentage of infection in both domestic and stray dogs. The prevalence of T. cruzi infection in dogs varies depending on ecological conditions, vector density, housing structures, and the effectiveness of vector control programs. In many rural environments, the prevalence of infection among dogs generally ranges between \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$7\%$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$30\%$$\end{document}, indicating persistent circulation of parasites within domestic transmission cycles37,38. A summary of the prevalence of infection reported in canine populations in different endemic regions is presented in Table 2. These statistics were compiled from field-based epidemiological investigations and veterinary surveillance studies.Table 2Reported prevalence of Trypanosoma cruzi infection in canine populations.RegionReported prevalence (%)SourceArgentina10 – 2836Brazil8 – 2138Mexico7 – 1939Bolivia12 – 3040Paraguay9 – 2441

Table 2Reported prevalence of Trypanosoma cruzi infection in canine populations.RegionReported prevalence (%)SourceArgentina10 – 2836Brazil8 – 2138Mexico7 – 1939Bolivia12 – 3040Paraguay9 – 2441

Reported prevalence of Trypanosoma cruzi infection in canine populations.

Longitudinal surveillance programs have also documented temporal variations in infection prevalence within canine populations. Representative observations from epidemiological surveys are summarized in Table 3. These data illustrate the persistence of infection within reservoir hosts and emphasize the epidemiological importance of dogs in the persistence of vector-mediated transmission cycles (Fig. 3).Table 3Observed prevalence of CCD in surveyed dog populations40,41.YearDogs TestedPositive CasesPrevalence (%)2010375287.520134505011.120166208313.4201990012013.32023120016013.3

In general, these observations highlight the critical role of canine reservoirs in the persistence and spatial propagation of T. cruzi, which justifies the incorporation of the dynamics of the canine host within the mathematical model.

Graphical simulation and discussion

In this section, numerical simulations are presented to illustrate the dynamical behavior of the delayed canine Chagas disease model and to compare the performance of the proposed first-order and second-order NSFD schemes. All simulations are carried out using MATLAB.

The parameter values used in the simulations are selected based on ranges reported in the scientific literature on Chagas disease and vector-borne epidemiological models. Since precise parameter estimates for canine Chagas disease are limited, the chosen values are intended to be biologically reasonable and are primarily used to investigate the qualitative behavior of the system rather than to provide exact quantitative predictions.
